# 2026-10-30

### 00:03 UTC — Actual native cubic transfer passes an eighteen-instance correctness set

#### What was done

Continued the user's requested native cold-transfer work in
`research/cubic_extension_transfer_20261025/native_end_to_end/`. The actual
Tian source-to-Jacobian construction, divisor-side normalization, eight-section
calibration, polynomial inversion, divisor recovery, sign alignment and target
IC now execute in native C++/FLINT/Singular and the pinned Rust backend.
Final-build full-transfer IC and target-rho controls pass on both originals;
the final records use one base-point choice each, with verification splits
recorded separately. Earlier successful three-/seven-choice records are retained.

Both original examples pass: p109 recovers1729 using one elliptic 3-isogeny;
p157 recovers271828 using two. Both verify on the original source. Sixteen more
public nine-line inputs were generated separately, eight per curve, with scalars
kept in separate receipts. All sixteen full solves and independent native Fp3
verifications pass in `results/panel-development-attempt2/`. Direct source rho
with and without negation also passes on both original examples. Original
constructed Jacobian orders are independently checked with nonzero prime-order
witnesses, the r+1 control and the exact genus-three Weil upper bound.

#### Decisions made

These are development correctness results; every record has benchmark=false.
No speedup, qualified wall time, universal chart totality or universal coverage
claim follows. Existing frozen sources/fixtures and all failed/interrupted
attempts remain unchanged. The target solver receives actual transported
divisors and no planted scalar. A partial binary verification is repaired using
bounded `(k+t)D-tD` arithmetic, without reducing by an assumed group order or
retrying a representable mismatch. New target-call counters include validation
and all verification retries; old stage counts retain their original scope.

#### Open threads

Publish the completed implementation/evidence in a regular PR. Finish
nuisance-seed checks, full native-work pricing, a frozen
controlled cold protocol and EKS/native-instrument integration before taking
qualified timings. No paid worker or default substitute fixture was submitted.
Root release-library validation remains unpassed after earlier resource-limited
attempts recorded by PR #2792; this change does not edit root Rust sources.

#### Errors

The original p157 assertion exposed an ambient-field square test: Fp nonsquares
become squares in degree eighteen. The corrected Fp Legendre predicate and an
explicit negative control pass. Actual inversion then exposed affine/projective
normalization and partial group-arithmetic charts; corrected normalization,
bounded base-point retries and scalar verification splits retain all rejections.

The first additional panel stopped on its second case: IC found the correct
candidate but its binary Jacobian verification was unavailable. Five scalar
verification splits repaired that exact case, independently verified as884834.
The repaired sixteen-case panel later paused at its mandatory checkpoint when
a depth-one main fetch hid the already-published base from Git ancestry. Stopped
that bundle, deepened commit metadata and checked that base17805c0d is a public
ancestor and the unpushed count is zero. The correctness-only resume verified
all input/binary hashes and completed missing phases without overwriting results.
The ancestry fetch warned about an inaccessible unrelated submodule; the
required commit graph was verified separately. A stopped development container

## 2026-10-30T03:10:00Z - ecdl-tensor-ic: port the coordinate-window factor bases from Go 8d17e6c

### What was done (commands, files, features)

Ported the Go commit `8d17e6c` ("Add hash-plus-coordinate-window factor bases,
with window-derived candidate generation") into `crates/ecdl-tensor-ic`. That
commit was authored after PR #2895 merged, so the Rust port was one feature
behind the Go prototype. The Go source is frozen and archived upstream as tags
`archive/ecdl-tensor-go-source-1c29f66` and
`archive/ecdl-tensor-go-source-8d17e6c`; the carrier branch was deleted.

- `src/field.rs`: `sqrt_mod` (Tonelli–Shanks), returning `Option<u64>`.
- `src/curve.rs`: `Curve::window_points(x_bound)`, enumerated by one modular
  square root per abscissa.
- `src/relation.rs`: `FactorBases::x_bound` / `window_bits`,
  `new_windowed_factor_bases(c, m, rng, k)` (with `k` lowered until the window
  fits the target size), `new_affine_factor_bases` delegating with `k = 0`,
  `list` dispatching to `list_all` / `list_window`, and `window_candidates`.
  `member` now also requires `x < x_bound[i]`.
- `src/pointgadget.rs`: `membership_check` adds a `range_check` on `x`, and
  `gadget_relation_network` takes its hint candidates from the window.
- `src/solver.rs`: `window_bits`; the gadget path builds
  `new_windowed_factor_bases`.
- `src/bin/ecdldemo.rs`: `--window K`.
- `tests/window_test.rs` (new, 7 tests) ports `TestSqrtMod`,
  `TestWindowedMembershipExhaustive`, `TestWindowCandidates`,
  `TestWindowedListMatchesFull`, `TestWindowedRelationCounts`, plus
  `k_zero_matches_affine_constructor` and a deterministic replacement for
  `BenchmarkFactorBaseBuild` that counts membership hashes via `opcount`.
- `README.md`, `PORTING.md`: the window, its gadgets and the identifier map.

Verified at `8d17e6c` with the local Go module first: `go vet ./...` exit 0,
`go test ./...` exit 0 (160.7 s). Rust: `cargo fmt --check` clean,
`cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test` 31 passed
(12 + 7 + 3 + 7 + 2). `ecdlscale -bits 64,256,1024` reproduces the Go table
unchanged (35.2 / 31.4 / 29.1 mul edges per bit, point 217.1 / 195.9 / 183.1,
max degree 352 / 1248 / 2304): the window is off by default. `ecdldemo -bits 8
-m 2 -gadgets -window 1 -seed 3` recovered xi = 79 and verified `[xi]P == Q`.

### Decisions made (and why)

Port the Go feature instead of adding more Go: the Rust crate is the upstream
home. Keep the default path bit-identical by delegating `k = 0` to the new
constructor; the seeded parity tests then stay meaningful. The window is a
deterministic constraint rather than another random hash, which is what makes
window-derived candidate generation possible; the trade-off (less independence
between F_i) is recorded in the README. Replace Go's wall-time benchmark with
an `opcount`-based deterministic test, so CI does not depend on timing.
Two trivial pre-existing clippy findings in the merged tests
(`nonminimal_bool`) were rewritten; the behaviour is identical.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Next: the speedup work, one focused PR each, in order: meet-in-the-middle
contraction (split the chain, join on the boundary point) with packed keys,

## 2026-10-30T03:40:00Z - ecdl-tensor-ic: meet-in-the-middle relation search

### What was done (commands, files, features)

Added a second elimination order for the relation network.
`relation::mitm_find_relation` enumerates the first half of the factor bases
into a table keyed by the partial sum and looks up the complement from the
second half, so one attempt costs about |F|^(m/2) + |F|^(m/2) point additions
(about 2 sqrt(2r)) instead of the forward contraction's |F_1| x ... x |F_m|
(about 2r). It returns one witness rather than a count; the solution set is
unchanged. `Solver::mitm` selects it, `ecdldemo -mitm` exposes it, and
`tests/mitm_test.rs` (3 tests) checks agreement with the network count, witness
validity, the addition-count reduction through `opcount`, and end-to-end solve.

Measured on the same seed (b = 12, m = 2, `-seed 5`, 131 relations):
`ecdldemo` took 32.174 s forward and 38 ms with `-mitm`, both recovering
xi = 1272. That is the expected sqrt(2r)-versus-r change, not a change in
asymptotics.

Verified: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets --
-D warnings` clean, `cargo test` 27 passed (12 + 7 + 3 + 3 + 2).

### Decisions made (and why)

Implement MITM as an opt-in elimination order rather than replacing the
contraction path: the crate's point is to demonstrate contraction, and the
forward path is what the ecbench arm measures. Assert the addition-count
reduction with `opcount` instead of wall time, so CI stays deterministic. No
asymptotic claim: 2 sqrt(2r) is still exponential, and the birthday bound makes
it the natural floor for a table-based decomposition of one target.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Next: packed table keys in the contraction (the string keys are the remaining
constant factor), then sparse linear algebra, then deterministic parallelism,
then m retuning as a counts-only preregistered experiment. The L2
tensor-vs-rho measurement from #2907 still has not run.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

The cost test first read a zero count because `opcount::start()` resets the
phase to `setup`; `set_phase` must follow `start` (both measurements were
fixed). The demo printed an empty network header for the MITM path; it now
says "meet-in-the-middle (no contraction)". Re-applied the two pre-existing
`clippy::nonminimal_bool` rewrites on this branch (the window branch carries
the same two lines, so the two changes agree).

## 2026-10-30T05:10:00Z - ecdl-tensor-ic: parallel relation search

### What was done (commands, files, features)

With meet-in-the-middle relation search the solver now draws a batch of
targets in order, then evaluates them on `Solver::threads` workers (0 = one per
available core, 1 = sequential; `ecdldemo -threads N`), and keeps the hits in
target order. The batch size depends only on the number of relations still
missing, so every thread count draws the same targets and the outcome is
identical. Target computation `[u]P + [v]Q` moved inside the workers, so only
the scalar draws are sequential. `opcount::active()` keeps the search on one
thread while a tally is running, since the tally is thread-local and the
ecbench arm's counts must stay complete.
`tests/parallel_relations_test.rs` (3 tests) checks 1-vs-4-thread equality
(secret, relation count, attempt count, factor-base size), that the tally path
stays charged, and prints the sequential/parallel timing.

Measured on release builds, b = 20, m = 2, `-seed 7`, by timing the phases:
factor base 156 ms, relation search 439 ms sequential vs 123 ms on 8 threads
(3.6x), sparse elimination 86 ms. Whole-solve phases 681 ms -> 365 ms (1.9x);
the remaining sequential part is factor-base listing, which is O(p) square
roots at toy sizes. `ecdldemo` wall time is dominated by toy curve generation
(naive point counting), not by the attack, and is not used as evidence.

Verified: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets --
-D warnings` clean, `cargo test` 35 passed (12 + 7 + 7 + 3 + 3 + 3).

### Decisions made (and why)

Determinism over load balance: target draws are fixed before dispatch and
results are collected in target order, so a replay reproduces the same
relations whatever the core count. Parallelism is skipped while tallying
instead of teaching the tally to aggregate, which keeps the ecbench arm's
accounting unchanged and deterministic. Scheduling parallelism after MITM
landed avoided stacking a third branch on an unmerged one.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Factor-base listing is now the largest sequential phase; the windowed
constructor already exists and could be enabled for the meet-in-the-middle path
(it is currently gadget-only). Then packed table keys and the m retuning
experiment. The L2 tensor-vs-rho measurement from #2907 still has not run.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

A first version compared 1 thread with 4 and failed: batching made the
sequential path diverge. Fixed by making the batch depend only on the remaining
count and always batching when MITM is on. A stale empty branch with the
intended name blocked `git checkout -b`; it was deleted and recreated. Phase
timing needed a temporary instrumented build, reverted afterwards.

## 2026-10-30T05:50:00Z - ecdl-tensor-ic: coordinate window on the meet-in-the-middle path

### What was done (commands, files, features)

The coordinate window is a constraint on the affine hash, not on the relation
encoding, so it no longer requires `gadgets`: the solver uses
`new_windowed_factor_bases` whenever `window_bits > 0`, and `ecdldemo -window K`
likewise. The meet-in-the-middle path gets the cheaper listing.
`tests/window_mitm_test.rs` (3 tests) covers a MITM solve with a window and no
gadgets, checks the solver really passes the window to the constructor, and
asserts through `opcount` that k = 8 evaluates at least 32x fewer membership
hashes than k = 0.

Measured listing cost (m = 2, one factor-base build):

| b | k = 0 | k = 8 | N at k = 8 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 20 | 29.7 ms | 0.6 ms | 1589 |
| 22 | 152 ms | 5.6 ms | 3615 |
| 24 | 859 ms | 12 ms | 7853 |

k = 0 grows about 4x per two bits (O(p) square roots); k = 8 is nearly flat.
Whole-demo time is not the evidence: at these sizes it is dominated by toy
curve generation.

Verified: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets --
-D warnings` clean, `cargo test` 35 passed.

### Decisions made (and why)

Enable the window independent of the gadget flag, because membership is the
only thing it changes. Keep `k = 0` the default so the existing parity tests
and the SHA-hash default path are untouched. Leave the choice of k to the
caller: larger k makes listing cheap but correlates the factor bases (the
window fixes the high bits of x), which is the trade-off already documented.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Packed table keys; a batched-inversion (Jacobian) meet-in-the-middle so the
sqrt(2r) additions stop paying one field inverse each; enabling the parallel
search on top of the window once #2934 lands; the m retuning experiment. The
L2 tensor-vs-rho measurement from #2907 still has not run.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

The first timing run passed `-threads`, which only exists on the unmerged #2934
branch, so the demo printed usage and exited; re-run without it. A direct
b = 24 demo is impractical because `random_prime_order_curve` counts points by
naive enumeration, so the factor-base cost was measured on a synthetic curve
with a prime modulus of the right size.

## 2026-10-30T03:10:00Z - ecdl-tensor-ic: port the coordinate-window factor bases from Go 8d17e6c

### What was done (commands, files, features)

Ported the Go commit `8d17e6c` ("Add hash-plus-coordinate-window factor bases,
with window-derived candidate generation") into `crates/ecdl-tensor-ic`. That
commit was authored after PR #2895 merged, so the Rust port was one feature
behind the Go prototype. The Go source is frozen and archived upstream as tags
`archive/ecdl-tensor-go-source-1c29f66` and
`archive/ecdl-tensor-go-source-8d17e6c`; the carrier branch was deleted.

- `src/field.rs`: `sqrt_mod` (Tonelli–Shanks), returning `Option<u64>`.
- `src/curve.rs`: `Curve::window_points(x_bound)`, enumerated by one modular
  square root per abscissa.
- `src/relation.rs`: `FactorBases::x_bound` / `window_bits`,
  `new_windowed_factor_bases(c, m, rng, k)` (with `k` lowered until the window
  fits the target size), `new_affine_factor_bases` delegating with `k = 0`,
  `list` dispatching to `list_all` / `list_window`, and `window_candidates`.
  `member` now also requires `x < x_bound[i]`.
- `src/pointgadget.rs`: `membership_check` adds a `range_check` on `x`, and
  `gadget_relation_network` takes its hint candidates from the window.
- `src/solver.rs`: `window_bits`; the gadget path builds
  `new_windowed_factor_bases`.
- `src/bin/ecdldemo.rs`: `--window K`.
- `tests/window_test.rs` (new, 7 tests) ports `TestSqrtMod`,
  `TestWindowedMembershipExhaustive`, `TestWindowCandidates`,
  `TestWindowedListMatchesFull`, `TestWindowedRelationCounts`, plus
  `k_zero_matches_affine_constructor` and a deterministic replacement for
  `BenchmarkFactorBaseBuild` that counts membership hashes via `opcount`.
- `README.md`, `PORTING.md`: the window, its gadgets and the identifier map.

Verified at `8d17e6c` with the local Go module first: `go vet ./...` exit 0,
`go test ./...` exit 0 (160.7 s). Rust: `cargo fmt --check` clean,
`cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test` 31 passed
(12 + 7 + 3 + 7 + 2). `ecdlscale -bits 64,256,1024` reproduces the Go table
unchanged (35.2 / 31.4 / 29.1 mul edges per bit, point 217.1 / 195.9 / 183.1,
max degree 352 / 1248 / 2304): the window is off by default. `ecdldemo -bits 8
-m 2 -gadgets -window 1 -seed 3` recovered xi = 79 and verified `[xi]P == Q`.

### Decisions made (and why)

Port the Go feature instead of adding more Go: the Rust crate is the upstream
home. Keep the default path bit-identical by delegating `k = 0` to the new
constructor; the seeded parity tests then stay meaningful. The window is a
deterministic constraint rather than another random hash, which is what makes
window-derived candidate generation possible; the trade-off (less independence
between F_i) is recorded in the README. Replace Go's wall-time benchmark with
an `opcount`-based deterministic test, so CI does not depend on timing.
Two trivial pre-existing clippy findings in the merged tests
(`nonminimal_bool`) were rewritten; the behaviour is identical.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Next: the speedup work, one focused PR each, in order: meet-in-the-middle
contraction (split the chain, join on the boundary point) with packed keys,
then sparse linear algebra, then deterministic parallelism, then m retuning as
a counts-only preregistered experiment. The L2 tensor-vs-rho measurement from
#2907 has still not run (it needs Iso Lab EKS on a `main` commit).

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

The first inserted `window_points` landed outside `impl Curve` (a brace
mis-count in the patch), and two of the new tests were wrong as first written:
one advanced a shared RNG with curve generation before comparing constructors,
and one demanded a 4x hash saving where `k = 1` only halves the window. All
three were fixed; the final suite is green. Local clippy 1.93 flags two
pre-existing test lints that CI's toolchain did not, handled as above.

## 2026-10-30T03:40:00Z - ecdl-tensor-ic: meet-in-the-middle relation search

### What was done (commands, files, features)

Added a second elimination order for the relation network.
`relation::mitm_find_relation` enumerates the first half of the factor bases
into a table keyed by the partial sum and looks up the complement from the
second half, so one attempt costs about |F|^(m/2) + |F|^(m/2) point additions
(about 2 sqrt(2r)) instead of the forward contraction's |F_1| x ... x |F_m|
(about 2r). It returns one witness rather than a count; the solution set is
unchanged. `Solver::mitm` selects it, `ecdldemo -mitm` exposes it, and
`tests/mitm_test.rs` (3 tests) checks agreement with the network count, witness
validity, the addition-count reduction through `opcount`, and end-to-end solve.

Measured on the same seed (b = 12, m = 2, `-seed 5`, 131 relations):
`ecdldemo` took 32.174 s forward and 38 ms with `-mitm`, both recovering
xi = 1272. That is the expected sqrt(2r)-versus-r change, not a change in
asymptotics.

Verified: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets --
-D warnings` clean, `cargo test` 27 passed (12 + 7 + 3 + 3 + 2).

### Decisions made (and why)

Implement MITM as an opt-in elimination order rather than replacing the
contraction path: the crate's point is to demonstrate contraction, and the
forward path is what the ecbench arm measures. Assert the addition-count
reduction with `opcount` instead of wall time, so CI stays deterministic. No
asymptotic claim: 2 sqrt(2r) is still exponential, and the birthday bound makes
it the natural floor for a table-based decomposition of one target.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Next: packed table keys in the contraction (the string keys are the remaining
constant factor), then sparse linear algebra, then deterministic parallelism,
then m retuning as a counts-only preregistered experiment. The L2
tensor-vs-rho measurement from #2907 still has not run.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

The cost test first read a zero count because `opcount::start()` resets the
phase to `setup`; `set_phase` must follow `start` (both measurements were
fixed). The demo printed an empty network header for the MITM path; it now
says "meet-in-the-middle (no contraction)". Re-applied the two pre-existing
`clippy::nonminimal_bool` rewrites on this branch (the window branch carries
the same two lines, so the two changes agree).

## 2026-10-30T05:10:00Z - ecdl-tensor-ic: parallel relation search

### What was done (commands, files, features)

With meet-in-the-middle relation search the solver now draws a batch of
targets in order, then evaluates them on `Solver::threads` workers (0 = one per
available core, 1 = sequential; `ecdldemo -threads N`), and keeps the hits in
target order. The batch size depends only on the number of relations still
missing, so every thread count draws the same targets and the outcome is
identical. Target computation `[u]P + [v]Q` moved inside the workers, so only
the scalar draws are sequential. `opcount::active()` keeps the search on one
thread while a tally is running, since the tally is thread-local and the
ecbench arm's counts must stay complete.
`tests/parallel_relations_test.rs` (3 tests) checks 1-vs-4-thread equality
(secret, relation count, attempt count, factor-base size), that the tally path
stays charged, and prints the sequential/parallel timing.

Measured on release builds, b = 20, m = 2, `-seed 7`, by timing the phases:
factor base 156 ms, relation search 439 ms sequential vs 123 ms on 8 threads
(3.6x), sparse elimination 86 ms. Whole-solve phases 681 ms -> 365 ms (1.9x);
the remaining sequential part is factor-base listing, which is O(p) square
roots at toy sizes. `ecdldemo` wall time is dominated by toy curve generation
(naive point counting), not by the attack, and is not used as evidence.

Verified: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets --
-D warnings` clean, `cargo test` 35 passed (12 + 7 + 7 + 3 + 3 + 3).

### Decisions made (and why)

Determinism over load balance: target draws are fixed before dispatch and
results are collected in target order, so a replay reproduces the same
relations whatever the core count. Parallelism is skipped while tallying
instead of teaching the tally to aggregate, which keeps the ecbench arm's
accounting unchanged and deterministic. Scheduling parallelism after MITM
landed avoided stacking a third branch on an unmerged one.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Factor-base listing is now the largest sequential phase; the windowed
constructor already exists and could be enabled for the meet-in-the-middle path
(it is currently gadget-only). Then packed table keys and the m retuning
experiment. The L2 tensor-vs-rho measurement from #2907 still has not run.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

A first version compared 1 thread with 4 and failed: batching made the
sequential path diverge. Fixed by making the batch depend only on the remaining
count and always batching when MITM is on. A stale empty branch with the
intended name blocked `git checkout -b`; it was deleted and recreated. Phase
timing needed a temporary instrumented build, reverted afterwards.

## 2026-10-30T05:50:00Z - ecdl-tensor-ic: coordinate window on the meet-in-the-middle path

### What was done (commands, files, features)

The coordinate window is a constraint on the affine hash, not on the relation
encoding, so it no longer requires `gadgets`: the solver uses
`new_windowed_factor_bases` whenever `window_bits > 0`, and `ecdldemo -window K`
likewise. The meet-in-the-middle path gets the cheaper listing.
`tests/window_mitm_test.rs` (3 tests) covers a MITM solve with a window and no
gadgets, checks the solver really passes the window to the constructor, and
asserts through `opcount` that k = 8 evaluates at least 32x fewer membership
hashes than k = 0.

Measured listing cost (m = 2, one factor-base build):

| b | k = 0 | k = 8 | N at k = 8 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 20 | 29.7 ms | 0.6 ms | 1589 |
| 22 | 152 ms | 5.6 ms | 3615 |
| 24 | 859 ms | 12 ms | 7853 |

k = 0 grows about 4x per two bits (O(p) square roots); k = 8 is nearly flat.
Whole-demo time is not the evidence: at these sizes it is dominated by toy
curve generation.

Verified: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets --
-D warnings` clean, `cargo test` 35 passed.

### Decisions made (and why)

Enable the window independent of the gadget flag, because membership is the
only thing it changes. Keep `k = 0` the default so the existing parity tests
and the SHA-hash default path are untouched. Leave the choice of k to the
caller: larger k makes listing cheap but correlates the factor bases (the
window fixes the high bits of x), which is the trade-off already documented.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Packed table keys; a batched-inversion (Jacobian) meet-in-the-middle so the
sqrt(2r) additions stop paying one field inverse each; enabling the parallel
search on top of the window once #2934 lands; the m retuning experiment. The
L2 tensor-vs-rho measurement from #2907 still has not run.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

The first timing run passed `-threads`, which only exists on the unmerged #2934
branch, so the demo printed usage and exited; re-run without it. A direct
b = 24 demo is impractical because `random_prime_order_curve` counts points by
naive enumeration, so the factor-base cost was measured on a synthetic curve
with a prime modulus of the right size.

## 2026-10-30T12:00:00Z - SAT/SMT prime-field IC solver design (branch feat/sat-smt-prime-field-solver-a42e87)

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- Read newest session log, the lead atlas brief and leads LEAD-ICE-sat-xor-pdp-oracle, LEAD-ICP-coppersmith-smallroot-semaev, `research/prime_field_smt_20261005/RESULT.md`, `research/pdp_smt_oracle_20261008/README.md`, RESEARCH_PRIME_FIELD_BREAKTHROUGH_PROGRAM.md §1-2, RESEARCH_SEMAEV_ROOTFIND_VS_GROEBNER.md.
- Wrote `research/sat_smt_prime_ic_design_20261030/DESIGN.md`: free-quotient-carry argument, LQ-CDCL (CDCL modulo a linearised-monomial lattice theory), analytic crossover, falsifiers. Probe script `probe.py` beside it.
- Local feasibility probe only (z3 5.1.0 binary, QF_BV bit-blast of Semaev S3, small-x bound B=2^k, one seed per cell, shared Mac, no isolation): m=2, n=20/28 bits, k=3..7; m=3 chain n=20. Results in DESIGN.md §6. Not an ecbench measurement; no S, speedup or rho ratio.

### Decisions made (and why)

- Disposition: no SAT/SMT formulation found that beats the pair-table oracle; LQ-CDCL gains at most p^{1/6} on the IC baseline (m=2) and the assumed lattice-call constant puts the break-even near p >= 2^100; recommended not to spend EKS time. Crossover numbers are analytic, inherit the reach figures from the breakthrough-program note, and the lattice-call cost L is an assumption.
- No isolab/EKS run, no leaderboard or benchmarks/ change (no validated measurement).

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

- Falsifier 2 (time fplll/sage LLL on the 9- and 49-dim S3 lattices vs a pair-table lookup) is the only step that could move the crossover; not run.
- Lead-atlas entry not filed; research-visuals graph/PDF not produced (design note, no experiment run claimed).

- Conductor control plane refused connection (127.0.0.1:8491); no reservation claimed, proceeded per documented absent-control-plane case.
- First probe script wrote to a bad path and z3 stat names were wrong (`:conflicts` vs `:sat-conflicts`); fixed and re-ran. The first background sweep chain stopped early; re-launched under nohup. z3 conflict counts are single-seed and non-monotone in B, so they do not support a clean scaling law.

## 2026-10-30T13:00:00Z - LQ-CDCL falsifier 2: lattice-call vs candidate timing

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- Sage launcher is broken (points at missing /Volumes/SSD990/cryptanalysis), no fpylll; used PARI/GP 2.17.3 `qflll` (`lll.gp`) and a C micro-benchmark (`lookup.c`, cc -O3, 62-bit Montgomery). Results in `research/sat_smt_prime_ic_design_20261030/timing_results.txt`, summarised in DESIGN.md §5.2.
- Dim-9 linearised S3 lattice: 0.156-0.661 ms/call (p = 60-256 bit). Dim-49 proxy (not the real shift lattice): 6.8-16.4 ms (mod p), 51-117 ms (mod p^3). Direct-subtraction candidate 49-123 ns; hash lookup 14-43 ns. Derived L = 1.3e3-1.3e4 (dim 9), 5.5e4-2.4e6 (dim 49); break-even log2 p* = 186-247 (level 1), 132-178 (level 3); 104-187 if L were 10x lower.

### Decisions made (and why)

- Falsifier 2 not falsified; LQ-CDCL stays "not measurable below ~2^100". No EKS/isolab run, no leaderboard or benchmarks/ change. Timings are single-run on a machine at load average ~21-23, so order of magnitude only.

## 2026-10-30T16:42:00Z - ecdb: Postgres schema and HTTP API for curves, Jacobians, isogenies, covers and transfers

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- User asked for a comprehensive RDS Postgres schema for elliptic curves and related objects (isogenies, Jacobians, hyperelliptic and higher genus, binary and Koblitz curves, subfields, Frobenius, polynomials, transfers) and an API agents can query. Built `ecdb/`: a standalone Rust crate (empty `[workspace]`, own Cargo.lock, outside the root build graph like `ecc2k130/dp-ingest`).
- Schema `ec` in eight immutable migrations (`ecdb/migrations/0001`-`0008`). Covers exact fields and number fields; curves of every genus and model; genus-1 invariants; hyperelliptic and ideal models; geometric invariants; aliases and standards; EC1 representations (preimage re-hashed by CHECK); abelian varieties and Jacobians (auto-created); points (Mumford included) and subgroups; Weil-polynomial isogeny classes (LMFDB-style labels for any q); Frobenius; End(E) and volcano levels; automorphisms; explicit endomorphisms; tau-adic data; descent; twists; pairings; p-adic invariants; isogenies, routes and volcanoes; typed relations; covers; DLP transfers; certificates; the polynomial store (division, modular, summation, class); global and local data; Galois images; generation; security; trait records; external labels; evidence links. Views and functions: `v_curve_summary`, `curve_document`, `resolve_curve`, `isogeny_neighbourhood`. Roles `ecdb_reader` and `ecdb_writer`.
- Importers (`ecdb import ...`): registry, the six `isogeny_routes.json` walk outputs, traits, covers, jacobian-transfers, level and Jacobian certificates, SQL-derived facts, and an optional LMFDB `av_fq_isog` export. Clean full import of this checkout into local Postgres 16: 188 s, 341 MB, 41,895 curves (21,080 genus 1, 20,642 genus 2, 173 genus 3), 1,809 isogeny classes from the repository plus 10 LMFDB sample rows, 478 kernel-certified isogenies, 687 routes, 20,817 covers, 61,305 transfer routes, 20 certificates. The only reported problem is 1 trait record whose slug has no curve row.
- Checks made during import, all passing on this checkout: ICV1 field hashes against moduli; model-JSON SHA-256 against slug suffixes; #E = q + 1 - t; Hasse; EC1 field and curve digests reproduced from preimages (1,023 of 1,023 registry representations); j computed from every registry model (Weierstrass, binary, Montgomery, Edwards, twisted Edwards) equal to the registry j (20,817 of 20,817); Delta = v^2 d_K by CHECK; LMFDB labels recomputed from Weil polynomials.
- HTTP API (axum): `/v1/meta`, `/v1/schema`, `/v1/curves` (about 30 filters), `/v1/curves/{ref}` (full document) and its isogenies, graph, transfers, covers and traits; isogenies; isogeny classes; fields; transfers; polynomials; `POST /v1/sql` (one read-only statement); token-gated `POST /v1/evidence` and `POST /v1/isogenies`. `openapi.yaml`, `README.md`, `docs/SCHEMA.md` (design, coverage of the brief, populated vs empty tables, sources checked), `Dockerfile`.
- Verification: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `cargo test` (11 unit tests, and the Postgres integration test `tests/api.rs` with ECDB_TEST_DATABASE_URL against a local `*_test` database). All pass. Manual curl checks: SQL containment (DML, data-modifying CTE, multiple statements and breakout refused; timeout fires; no advisory lock survives a request) and the write endpoints.
- `scripts/check_curve_names.py`: added `ecdb/src/math.rs` to SELF, the list of tools that recognise retired names (its tests quote them). The full lint reports only 2 pre-existing findings in `docs/session-logs/2026-10-26.md`.

### Decisions made (and why)

- Home is `crypto/ecdb`: the registry, traits, covers, transfers and certificates it loads live in `docs/curves/`. Rust, not Python, per AGENTS.md "Implementation language". A standalone crate keeps axum and tokio-postgres out of the root lock file.
- Reused ICV1 and EC1 identities and the traits.json status vocabulary instead of a parallel convention. No equivalence is inferred from j, order or degree. The Montgomery and Edwards models of one curve share a Weierstrass model, and so share a cover; they are not linked automatically.
- Groups, points and isogenies attach to abelian varieties (every curve gets its Jacobian), so genus 2+ (Mumford divisors, Richelot) shares the tables with genus 1.
- Transfer costs are stored as the producer's formula estimates with the cost model in the provenance parameters, status by evidence class, and a caution in the API. They are leads, not measured speedups. Derived rho estimates are status `heuristic` and explicitly not matched ecbench references.
- No experiment was run; resume-checkpoint, research-visuals, benchmarks and leaderboard rules do not apply to this infrastructure change.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

- Tables without a producer yet: division / modular / summation / class polynomials, geometric invariants of the cover curves, GLV endomorphisms and decompositions, group structure, p-adic invariants, typed twist and model relations, volcano components, global / local / Galois data from LMFDB `ec_curvedata` and `g2c_curves`, field embeddings.
- Deployment to an actual RDS instance and an MCP wrapper over the endpoints are not done.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

- The disk allowance was exhausted by the repository checkouts mid-build ("No space left on device"). Removed the uv cache and the partial target directory, then built and ran the throwaway Postgres cluster under /dev/shm (scratch, not deliverables).
- The live LMFDB API returned a captcha page. A user-agent retry was blocked as bot-check circumvention and not repeated. Used the local LMFDB extracts and web-search results of the inventory pages instead; recalled column names are labelled as recalled in docs/SCHEMA.md.
- Fixed during testing: domain-typed bind parameters (now cast through text); field `order_bits` (now ceil(log2 q), it was off by one for 2^m); agent sources colliding on the source natural key (now keyed by `agent:<name>` uri); numeric scale on powers of 2 in derived tau rows (trunc / div); session advisory locks surviving `/v1/sql` (DISCARD ALL after each request).
- Conductor CLI absent (the documented warn/allow case); no reservation claimed.

Wrote a scoped managed policy (`ecdb/deploy/iam-policy.json`: us-west-2 only, `ecdb*` names or `project=ecdb` tags, default KMS keys via RDS and Lambda, PassRole for `ecdb-lambda-exec` only) and the admin commands. - Added `ecdb lambda api|admin` (`src/lambda.rs`, lambda_http and lambda_runtime 1.x). The admin actions are status, migrate, bootstrap_roles (logins `ecdb_api` / `ecdb_api_writer`, passwords quoted through format('%L'), each a member of one group role) and import <selection>. Also added `import::run` with `Selection`, shared by the CLI and Lambda, and gzip fallback in `read_json`: `<file>.gz` is read when `<file>` is absent, and the recorded SHA-256 stays that of the uncompressed bytes.
- `ecdb/deploy/`: `build-lambda.sh` (static x86_64 musl build, bootstrap, RDS us-west-2 CA bundle, gzipped catalogues; 12 MB zip), `deploy-aws.sh` (idempotent: default VPC, ecdb-lambda / ecdb-rds security groups with 5432-only rules, subnet group, `ecdb/master` and `ecdb/api` secrets, RDS PG16 db.t4g.medium private and encrypted with deletion protection, two VPC Lambdas, function URL, then migrate, roles, every importer and status), `README.md`.
- Verified locally: the gnu release binary needs GLIBC_2.39 through a non-weak version need (Lambda AL2023 has 2.34), so the build is static musl (static-pie, 15 MB). The musl binary migrated and imported everything from the gzipped package data in 90 s (21,080 curves, the same single trait-slug problem), and recorded the registry sha 88390d7b4720... of the uncompressed file. Both Lambda modes ran against a local stand-in for the Lambda Runtime API:
  - admin: status / migrate / bootstrap_roles / import registry (9 s) / status, and a clean error for an unknown action;
  - api: function-URL v2 events returned /healthz 200, 401 without the token, curve document and listing 200, `/v1/sql` positional rows.
- fmt, clippy -D warnings, 11 unit tests and the Postgres integration test pass.

### Decisions made (and why)

- Lambda rather than ECS or EC2: a function URL gives agents HTTPS without a domain, certificate or load balancer, and the import fits inside the 15-minute limit per importer. Catalogues ship gzipped in the package, so the VPC functions need no NAT or S3 endpoint, and the Lambda security group allows only 5432 to RDS.
- Separate RDS instance `ecdb`; `rho-dp` (ECC2K-130 distinguished points) is left untouched.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

- Apply the IAM grant (admin), then `ecdb/deploy/build-lambda.sh` + `ecdb/deploy/deploy-aws.sh`, smoke-test the function URL from here, and report the endpoint and secret names.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

- Two worker restarts wiped /dev/shm (build directory, local Postgres, the rustup copy); each was recreated. `pkill -f` with a pattern that also matched the running shell killed the shell twice; switched to pid files and fresh ports. The root disk was too full for the rustup musl target (No space left on device), so the toolchain was copied to /dev/shm and the target added there.

### 23:10 UTC — Runtime relay (Claude Code ↔ Codex) installed

#### What was done
- Installed `scripts/relay.py`, `docs/runtime-relay.md` and `tests/test_runtime_relay.py` from
  isogeny-labs/crypto-autoresearcher (merged as PR #2627). Wired the Claude Code hooks
  `SessionStart`, `Stop` and `StopFailure` and the Codex hooks `SessionStart` and `Stop`
  (`.claude/settings.json`, `.codex/hooks.json`) next to the existing Conductor hooks. Taught
  `scripts/checkpoint.sh` to carry `.resume/{HANDOFF.md,handoff.json,relay.jsonl}`, and added
  a pointer in AGENTS.md.
- `python3 -m pytest -q tests/test_runtime_relay.py`: 14 passed. `bash -n scripts/checkpoint.sh`
  passes. `relay.py doctor`: the only gap is that the `codex` CLI is not installed in this container.
- Verified live: a `StopFailure` with `rate_limit` (on this session's real transcript) marked
  the handoff pending for Codex and pushed checkpoint
  `s3://setec-astronomy-artifacts/checkpoints/crypto/claude__runtime-relay-20261030/20261030T230755Z-11173ecd0230/`.
  The Codex `SessionStart` hook announced the handoff, and `accept` claimed it.
- Verified live in cryptanalysis-rl: `relay.py run --prefer codex,claude` with a stand-in Codex
  that printed a usage-limit message handed off to a real `claude -p`. That session ran
  `accept` and reported the branch, HEAD and dirty-file count from the handoff.

#### Decisions made
- The prompt is passed after `--`. The first live run showed that
  `claude -p --allowedTools A B <prompt>` consumes the prompt as a tool name.
- The file is kept byte-identical to the canonical copy so `relay.py install` stays a plain copy.

#### Open threads
- Codex has not been exercised with a real binary: no `codex` CLI or OpenAI credentials here.
  Run `python3 scripts/relay.py doctor` on a host that has both CLIs.
- The `--` fix also goes back to crypto-autoresearcher in its own PR.

#### Errors
- First live run: `claude -p` exited with "Input must be provided" because a multi-value
  `--allowedTools` swallowed the prompt. Fixed with the `--` separator and a regression test.
- Conductor CLI is not installed in this container, so `conductor check` could not run; the
  hooks warn and let edits through (CLAUDE.md).

## 2026-10-30T23:30:00Z - ecdb deployed to AWS (RDS PostgreSQL 16 + Lambda, us-west-2)

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- The user created role `ecdb-lambda-exec` (AWSLambdaVPCAccessExecutionRole) and attached managed policy `ecdb-deploy` (= `ecdb/deploy/iam-policy.json`) to IAM user `claude`; afterwards `ec2:DescribeVpcs` and `rds:DescribeDBInstances` succeed. Built `ecdb/deploy/build-lambda.sh` from main acf27a37 (12 MB zip, SOURCE_COMMIT acf27a37aafe) and ran `ecdb/deploy/deploy-aws.sh` three times. All three runs are idempotent; the first two stopped on script bugs fixed in this branch.
- Created in account 104186811093, us-west-2:
  - security groups `ecdb-lambda` (sg-048ac5a7a695c92be; egress only tcp 5432 to ecdb-rds) and `ecdb-rds` (sg-085d9bab2bd6f35c0; ingress only tcp 5432 from ecdb-lambda), both verified with describe-security-group-rules;
  - DB subnet group `ecdb` (4 default-VPC subnets) and secrets `ecdb/master` and `ecdb/api`;
  - RDS `ecdb`: PostgreSQL 16.13, db.t4g.medium, 20 GB gp3, not public, encrypted, deletion protection, CA rds-ca-rsa2048-g1; endpoint ecdb.cn266os0m3am.us-west-2.rds.amazonaws.com, available at 23:15:27Z;
  - Lambda functions `ecdb-admin` and `ecdb-api` (provided.al2023, VPC), and the function URL https://l6zg6lz4dr45t75p5qn5aocnme0pckrm.lambda-url.us-west-2.on.aws/ (auth NONE; the app checks the bearer token from `ecdb/api` read_token).
- Admin results:
  - migrate applied 1-8; bootstrap_roles created `ecdb_api` (member of ecdb_reader only) and `ecdb_api_writer` (member of ecdb_writer only);
  - imports with no problems except the known 1 unmatched trait slug: registry 20 s, walks, traits 47 s, covers 49 s, transfers 11 s, certificates, derived;
  - status: 41,895 curves, 1,809 isogeny classes, 478 isogenies, 20,817 curve morphisms, 61,305 DLP transfers, 13 sources, 389 MB.
- Smoke test from this container over the public URL: /healthz 200 (1.4 s cold); /v1/curves/secp256k1 401 without the token and 200 with it (j 0, d_K -3, End maximal); Koblitz filter total 10; P-256 graph depth 2 with 84 nodes and 156 edges; /v1/sql genus counts 21,080 / 20,642 / 173; a data-modifying CTE refused (SQLSTATE 0A000).

### Decisions made (and why)

- Reserved concurrency for ecdb-api is best-effort. AWS rejected the reservation of 10 because the account must keep 10 unreserved executions, so the account-wide limit bounds connections instead. The policy grants no lambda:GetAccountSettings, so the exact limit was not read.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

- PR with the three deploy-script fixes. Optional follow-ups: a custom domain, IAM auth on the URL, rotating the tokens through `ecdb/api`.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

The original PR branch disappeared because #2959 merged and was deleted during
review; the attempted branch clone was retried from current main. The prior
prose mixed relation and verification cost and gave an inconsistent ratio.
This follow-up records the correction rather than rewriting measured evidence.
